Hello, Doctor. Thank you so much for taking the time to see me. I, um... I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time, as it's probably nothing major. But... well, I just can't seem to turn off my worrying once it starts. 

It's a bit silly, really. I mean, I'll sit down, I'll make my lists, I'll double-check all the dates for whatever is coming up, and I'll do absolutely everything I reasonably can to handle things. But even after all that, my mind just keeps fretting over it and I can't seem to shut it off. 

I hope I'm explaining that right... does that make sense?

I also help with some local exhibition planning, and I've actually started turning down certain parts of that. Not because I don't care! Oh, well... I suppose I do get a little stiff, but I really think that's just normal for someone my age, you know? I wouldn't want you to think it's anything highly unusual or that I'm complaining about regular aches and pains.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... I try very hard not to be a "worrywart," as they say. I wouldn't want you to think I sit around all day imagining the worst! I really do try to just stay positive and organized. 

But... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, my mind does tend to wander a bit more than I'd like. Not about anything catastrophic, I promise! Just... well, everyday responsibilities. It's like my mind keeps roaming. I'll be thinking about the exhibition schedule, and then before I know it, I'm fretting over my daughter's travel plans, and then suddenly I'm wondering if the house gutters need fixing. 

It sounds so silly when I say it out loud... but sometimes these small things just start to feel as if they could unravel into something much bigger if I don't stay on top of them. I just like to be prepared and make sure everything is taken care of. That's just being responsible, isn't it, Doctor? I hope I don't sound too foolish...

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... I'm not entirely sure how much other people worry, to be perfectly honest. I don't really go around comparing notes with my friends on that sort of thing! I wouldn't want to pry into their private thoughts, and I certainly wouldn't want them thinking I'm overly anxious or... or dramatic. 

I suppose... maybe I just have a more active mind? I think some people are just naturally more conscientious, don't you? I like to think of it as being thoughtful rather than, you know, being a worrier. I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing and that everyone is taken care of. 

So, I don't know if it's *more* than most people. I just try to be thorough. I hope that's a helpful answer, Doctor? I'm not really sure how to measure it against anyone else...

I just like to be prepared and make sure everything is taken care of. That's just being responsible, isn't it, Doctor? I hope I don't sound too foolish...\n\nDoctor: Has your worrying affected your ability to complete tasks at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Oh, I wouldn't say it's affecting my ability to complete things, really. I mean, I'm certainly not dropping the ball on my main responsibilities! I still keep up with the garden, and I never miss my book club, of course. I'd hate for you to think I'm just letting everything slide. \n\nBut... well, I suppose things do take a little more effort lately. I've noticed I'm arriving at book club a bit less prepared than I usually am—just not quite finishing the reading, which is so unlike me. And, um... with the exhibition planning I mentioned, I've actually had to turn down a few of the tasks they asked me to help with. It just... it feels a bit harder to manage all the moving parts than it used to. \n\nI just didn't want to overcommit and let anyone down, you see. I thought it was better to step back slightly rather than do a poor job. That makes sense, doesn't it? I hope you don't think I'm just becoming lazy, Doctor...\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you worry about things more than most people?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I'm not entirely sure how much other people worry, to be perfectly honest. I don't really go around comparing notes with my friends on that sort of thing! I wouldn't want to pry into their private thoughts, and I certainly wouldn't want them thinking I'm overly anxious or... or dramatic. \n\nI suppose... maybe I just have a more active mind? I think some people are just naturally more conscientious, don't you? I like to think of it as being thoughtful rather than, you know, being a worrier. I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing and that everyone is taken care of. \n\nSo, I don't know if it's *more* than most people. I just try to be thorough. I hope that's a helpful answer, Doctor? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, no, I wouldn't say I have anything like that. I mean, certainly not... not like a broken record or anything disturbing! I know some people get those awful, intrusive thoughts, but I don't have anything quite so dramatic. 

I just... well, my mind just flits from one practical concern to another, like the gutters or the exhibition. It's not that I'm stuck on one terrifying image or anything. I can usually distract myself if I really need to, like if I sit down with a good book or focus on my gardening. 

I just wouldn't want you to think I'm losing touch with reality or obsessing over things, Doctor. It's really just ordinary, everyday worries, just... perhaps a few too many of them at once. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining the difference clearly enough...

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello, Doctor. Thank you so much for taking the time to see me. I, um... I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time, as it's probably nothing major. But... well, I just can't seem to turn off my worrying once it starts. \n\nIt's a bit silly, really. I mean, I'll sit down, I'll make my lists, I'll double-check all the dates for whatever is coming up, and I'll do absolutely everything I reasonably can to handle things. But even after all that, my mind just keeps fretting over it and I can't seem to shut it off. \n\nI hope I'm explaining that right... does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a sudden, intense feeling of fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. Not like a sudden attack or anything like that. I mean, I get nervous, of course, but it's more of a... a slow, steady hum in the background, rather than a big explosion of fear. \n\nI wouldn't want you to think I'm having, you know, those sort of dramatic episodes. I really try to keep things together. Is... is that the sort of thing you were looking for? I hope I'm answering that clearly enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or activities because of your worrying?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I wouldn't say I completely avoid things. I really try my best to stay active and keep up with my commitments, I promise. I still keep up with the garden, of course, and I always go to my book club. I would absolutely hate to let anyone down. \n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, I have been arriving to book club a little less prepared lately. And, um... I also help with some local exhibition planning, and I've actually started turning down certain parts of that. Not because I don't care! It's just... well, it feels a little harder to manage than it used to, and I'd hate to make a mess of it for everyone else because my mind is so preoccupied. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound terribly incapable or lazy. I really do try to stay on top of things. Does that... does that answer your question, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose \"on edge\" might be a bit too strong of a word. I wouldn't want to overstate things and make it sound worse than it is. But... yes, I suppose I do find myself feeling a little wound up sometimes, even when I'm just trying to relax in my own living room. \n\nI'll notice that I'm tapping my fingers the whole time I'm on a phone call with a friend. Or I'll keep getting up to straighten a picture frame or tidy up the coffee table. Not because the house is a mess, I promise! It's just because... well, sitting perfectly quietly seems to make me feel a tiny bit jumpy lately. \n\nIt's nothing terrible, really, I just have a hard time staying entirely still. I hope that doesn't make me sound completely neurotic... I just like to keep things neat, you know? Does that sound like the sort of restlessness you were asking about, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself feeling tense in your muscles, like your shoulders or neck are tight?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I do get a little stiff, but I really think that's just normal for someone my age, you know? I wouldn't want you to think it's anything highly unusual or that I'm complaining about regular aches and pains. \n\nBut... if I really think about it, I guess by the evening, my shoulders and neck do get awfully tight. It's almost like I've been bracing myself all day without realizing it. And, um... sometimes when I sit down to read or work on the plans for the garden center, I'll suddenly notice that I'm clenching my jaw quite hard. \n\nI try to stretch it out, of course, and I don't let it stop me from getting things done. I hope that's not too unusual? Is that the kind of tension you mean, Doctor, or am I just describing normal getting-older stiffness?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself avoiding social interactions because of your worrying?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no... I don't think I avoid people. I really do love seeing my friends, and I wouldn't want you to think I'm becoming some sort of recluse. I still go to my book club, like I mentioned, and I always make sure to call my sister on Sundays. I'd feel just awful if I let those relationships slip.\n\nI mean... I suppose there are times when I might decline a larger dinner party if I'm feeling particularly overwhelmed by everything else on my plate. But that's just because I wouldn't want to be poor company, you know? I'd hate to bring down the mood if my mind is racing about other things. \n\nBut overall, no, I really try my very best to stay connected. I hope that's the right answer? I just want to be sure I'm giving you a clear picture, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about everyday things, such as finances, health, or relationships, even when there's no apparent reason to worry?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I try very hard not to be a \"worrywart,\" as they say. I wouldn't want you to think I sit around all day imagining the worst! I really do try to just stay positive and organized. \n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, my mind does tend to wander a bit more than I'd like. Not about anything catastrophic, I promise! Just... well, everyday responsibilities. It's like my mind keeps roaming. I'll be thinking about the exhibition schedule, and then before I know it, I'm fretting over my daughter's travel plans, and then suddenly I'm wondering if the house gutters need fixing. \n\nIt sounds so silly when I say it out loud... but sometimes these small things just start to feel as if they could unravel into something much bigger if I don't stay on top of them. I just like to be prepared and make sure everything is taken care of. That's just being responsible, isn't it, Doctor? I hope I don't sound too foolish...\n\nDoctor: Has your worrying affected your ability to complete tasks at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Oh, I wouldn't say it's affecting my ability to complete things, really. I mean, I'm certainly not dropping the ball on my main responsibilities! I still keep up with the garden, and I never miss my book club, of course. I'd hate for you to think I'm just letting everything slide. \n\nBut... well, I suppose things do take a little more effort lately. I've noticed I'm arriving at book club a bit less prepared than I usually am—just not quite finishing the reading, which is so unlike me. And, um... with the exhibition planning I mentioned, I've actually had to turn down a few of the tasks they asked me to help with. It just... it feels a bit harder to manage all the moving parts than it used to. \n\nI just didn't want to overcommit and let anyone down, you see. I thought it was better to step back slightly rather than do a poor job. That makes sense, doesn't it? I hope you don't think I'm just becoming lazy, Doctor...\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you worry about things more than most people?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I'm not entirely sure how much other people worry, to be perfectly honest. I don't really go around comparing notes with my friends on that sort of thing! I wouldn't want to pry into their private thoughts, and I certainly wouldn't want them thinking I'm overly anxious or... or dramatic. \n\nI suppose... maybe I just have a more active mind? I think some people are just naturally more conscientious, don't you? I like to think of it as being thoughtful rather than, you know, being a worrier. I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing and that everyone is taken care of. \n\nSo, I don't know if it's *more* than most people. I just try to be thorough. I hope that's a helpful answer, Doctor? I'm not really sure how to measure it against anyone else...\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of things in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, yes... I suppose I touched on this a little bit earlier. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, Doctor! But, um, yes, it does seem to cover quite a few different areas. \n\nLike I mentioned before, my mind just sort of keeps roaming around. One minute it's the exhibition schedule, and then it jumps to my daughter's travel plans, and then suddenly I'm worrying over whether the house gutters need fixing. I hope I don't sound too foolish...\n\nDoctor: Has your worrying affected your ability to complete tasks at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Oh, I wouldn't say it's affecting my ability to complete things, really. I mean, I'm certainly not dropping the ball on my main responsibilities! I still keep up with the garden, and I never miss my book club, of course. I'd hate for you to think I'm just letting everything slide. \n\nBut... well, I suppose things do take a little more effort lately. I've noticed I'm arriving at book club a bit less prepared than I usually am—just not quite finishing the reading, which is so unlike me. I mean, I'll sit down, I'll make my lists, I'll double-check all the dates for whatever is coming up, and I'll do absolutely everything I reasonably can to handle things. Not like a sudden attack or anything like that. I also help with some local exhibition planning, and I've actually started turning down certain parts of that. Not because I don't care! I really do try to stay on top of things. Does that... does that answer your question, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose \"on edge\" might be a bit too strong of a word. I wouldn't want to overstate things and make it sound worse than it is. But... yes, I suppose I do find myself feeling a little wound up sometimes, even when I'm just trying to relax in my own living room. \n\nI'll notice that I'm tapping my fingers the whole time I'm on a phone call with a friend. Or I'll keep getting up to straighten a picture frame or tidy up the coffee table. Not because the house is a mess, I promise! It's just because... well, sitting perfectly quietly seems to make me feel a tiny bit jumpy lately. \n\nIt's nothing terrible, really, I just have a hard time staying entirely still. I hope that doesn't make me sound completely neurotic... I just like to keep things neat, you know? Does that sound like the sort of restlessness you were asking about, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself feeling tense in your muscles, like your shoulders or neck are tight?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... It's like my mind keeps roaming. I just like to be prepared and make sure everything is taken care of. That's just being responsible, isn't it, Doctor? I'd hate for you to think I'm just letting everything slide. \n\nBut... well, I suppose things do take a little more effort lately. I've noticed I'm arriving at book club a bit less prepared than I usually am—just not quite finishing the reading, which is so unlike me. I thought it was better to step back slightly rather than do a poor job. That makes sense, doesn't it? I wouldn't want to pry into their private thoughts, and I certainly wouldn't want them thinking I'm overly anxious or... or dramatic. \n\nI suppose... maybe I just have a more active mind? Oh, no, I wouldn't say that's a problem for me. I mean, I enjoy social situations! I have my friends from the gallery, and my book club... I'm quite comfortable around them.

I just like to be prepared and make sure everything is taken care of. That's just being responsible, isn't it, Doctor? I hope I don't sound too foolish...\n\nDoctor: Has your worrying affected your ability to complete tasks at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Oh, I wouldn't say it's affecting my ability to complete things, really. I mean, I'm certainly not dropping the ball on my main responsibilities! I still keep up with the garden, and I never miss my book club, of course. I'd hate for you to think I'm just letting everything slide. \n\nBut... well, I suppose things do take a little more effort lately. I've noticed I'm arriving at book club a bit less prepared than I usually am—just not quite finishing the reading, which is so unlike me. It just... it feels a bit harder to manage all the moving parts than it used to. \n\nI just didn't want to overcommit and let anyone down, you see. I thought it was better to step back slightly rather than do a poor job. That makes sense, doesn't it? I hope you don't think I'm just becoming lazy, Doctor...\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you worry about things more than most people?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I'm not entirely sure how much other people worry, to be perfectly honest. I don't really go around comparing notes with my friends on that sort of thing! I just want to make sure I'm handling things the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about being judged or evaluated by others in social situations?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that's a problem for me. I mean, I enjoy social situations! I have my friends from the gallery, and my book club... I'm quite comfortable around them. \n\nI suppose, like anyone, I like to make a good impression. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I suppose... perhaps a little bit? I really try not to let things get out of hand, I promise. It's just that sometimes, my mind keeps roaming from one thing to the next. Like, I'll be thinking about the exhibition schedule at the gallery, and then suddenly I'm worrying about my daughter's travel plans, and then wondering if the house gutters need fixing before it rains... 

I know they're just normal, everyday things, but sometimes they all sort of pile up. And then, well, these small things start to feel as if they could unravel into something much bigger, if that makes sense? 

I really don't want to sound like I'm making a mountain out of a molehill, Doctor. I know I should just take things one step at a time. Do you think it's terribly unusual to get a bit overwhelmed by all those little details? I just want to be sure I'm keeping a proper perspective on things.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF41.1, F41.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello, Doctor. Thank you so much for taking the time to see me. I, um... I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time, as it's probably nothing major. But... well, I just can't seem to turn off my worrying once it starts. \n\nIt's a bit silly, really. I mean, I'll sit down, I'll make my lists, I'll double-check all the dates for whatever is coming up, and I'll do absolutely everything I reasonably can to handle things. But even after all that, my mind just keeps fretting over it and I can't seem to shut it off. \n\nI hope I'm explaining that right... does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a sudden, intense feeling of fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. Not like a sudden attack or anything like that. I mean, I get nervous, of course, but it's more of a... a slow, steady hum in the background, rather than a big explosion of fear. \n\nI wouldn't want you to think I'm having, you know, those sort of dramatic episodes. I really try to keep things together. Is... is that the sort of thing you were looking for? I hope I'm answering that clearly enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or activities because of your worrying?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I wouldn't say I completely avoid things. I really try my best to stay active and keep up with my commitments, I promise. I still keep up with the garden, of course, and I always go to my book club. I would absolutely hate to let anyone down. \n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, I have been arriving to book club a little less prepared lately. And, um... I also help with some local exhibition planning, and I've actually started turning down certain parts of that. Not because I don't care! It's just... well, it feels a little harder to manage than it used to, and I'd hate to make a mess of it for everyone else because my mind is so preoccupied. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound terribly incapable or lazy. I really do try to stay on top of things. Does that... does that answer your question, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose \"on edge\" might be a bit too strong of a word. I wouldn't want to overstate things and make it sound worse than it is. But... yes, I suppose I do find myself feeling a little wound up sometimes, even when I'm just trying to relax in my own living room. \n\nI'll notice that I'm tapping my fingers the whole time I'm on a phone call with a friend. Or I'll keep getting up to straighten a picture frame or tidy up the coffee table. Not because the house is a mess, I promise! It's just because... well, sitting perfectly quietly seems to make me feel a tiny bit jumpy lately. \n\nIt's nothing terrible, really, I just have a hard time staying entirely still. I hope that doesn't make me sound completely neurotic... I just like to keep things neat, you know? Does that sound like the sort of restlessness you were asking about, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself feeling tense in your muscles, like your shoulders or neck are tight?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I do get a little stiff, but I really think that's just normal for someone my age, you know? I wouldn't want you to think it's anything highly unusual or that I'm complaining about regular aches and pains. \n\nBut... if I really think about it, I guess by the evening, my shoulders and neck do get awfully tight. It's almost like I've been bracing myself all day without realizing it. And, um... sometimes when I sit down to read or work on the plans for the garden center, I'll suddenly notice that I'm clenching my jaw quite hard. \n\nI try to stretch it out, of course, and I don't let it stop me from getting things done. I hope that's not too unusual? Is that the kind of tension you mean, Doctor, or am I just describing normal getting-older stiffness?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself avoiding social interactions because of your worrying?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no... I don't think I avoid people. I really do love seeing my friends, and I wouldn't want you to think I'm becoming some sort of recluse. I still go to my book club, like I mentioned, and I always make sure to call my sister on Sundays. I'd feel just awful if I let those relationships slip.\n\nI mean... I suppose there are times when I might decline a larger dinner party if I'm feeling particularly overwhelmed by everything else on my plate. But that's just because I wouldn't want to be poor company, you know? I'd hate to bring down the mood if my mind is racing about other things. \n\nBut overall, no, I really try my very best to stay connected. I hope that's the right answer? I just want to be sure I'm giving you a clear picture, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about everyday things, such as finances, health, or relationships, even when there's no apparent reason to worry?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I try very hard not to be a \"worrywart,\" as they say. I wouldn't want you to think I sit around all day imagining the worst! I really do try to just stay positive and organized. \n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, my mind does tend to wander a bit more than I'd like. Not about anything catastrophic, I promise! Just... well, everyday responsibilities. It's like my mind keeps roaming. I'll be thinking about the exhibition schedule, and then before I know it, I'm fretting over my daughter's travel plans, and then suddenly I'm wondering if the house gutters need fixing. \n\nIt sounds so silly when I say it out loud... but sometimes these small things just start to feel as if they could unravel into something much bigger if I don't stay on top of them. I just like to be prepared and make sure everything is taken care of. That's just being responsible, isn't it, Doctor? I hope I don't sound too foolish...\n\nDoctor: Has your worrying affected your ability to complete tasks at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Oh, I wouldn't say it's affecting my ability to complete things, really. I mean, I'm certainly not dropping the ball on my main responsibilities! I still keep up with the garden, and I never miss my book club, of course. I'd hate for you to think I'm just letting everything slide. \n\nBut... well, I suppose things do take a little more effort lately. I've noticed I'm arriving at book club a bit less prepared than I usually am—just not quite finishing the reading, which is so unlike me. And, um... with the exhibition planning I mentioned, I've actually had to turn down a few of the tasks they asked me to help with. It just... it feels a bit harder to manage all the moving parts than it used to. \n\nI just didn't want to overcommit and let anyone down, you see. I thought it was better to step back slightly rather than do a poor job. That makes sense, doesn't it? I hope you don't think I'm just becoming lazy, Doctor...\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you worry about things more than most people?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I'm not entirely sure how much other people worry, to be perfectly honest. I don't really go around comparing notes with my friends on that sort of thing! I wouldn't want to pry into their private thoughts, and I certainly wouldn't want them thinking I'm overly anxious or... or dramatic. \n\nI suppose... maybe I just have a more active mind? I think some people are just naturally more conscientious, don't you? I like to think of it as being thoughtful rather than, you know, being a worrier. I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing and that everyone is taken care of. \n\nSo, I don't know if it's *more* than most people. I just try to be thorough. I hope that's a helpful answer, Doctor? I'm not really sure how to measure it against anyone else...\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of things in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, yes... I suppose I touched on this a little bit earlier. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, Doctor! But, um, yes, it does seem to cover quite a few different areas. \n\nLike I mentioned before, my mind just sort of keeps roaming around. One minute it's the exhibition schedule, and then it jumps to my daughter's travel plans, and then suddenly I'm worrying over whether the house gutters need fixing. So I guess that is a fairly wide range of topics, isn't it? \n\nIt's just that... well, all those little things from different parts of my life start to feel as if they could unravel into something much bigger if I'm not careful to keep track of them. I just want to make sure everything is running smoothly and everyone is okay. Does that answer your question properly?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts or mental images that you can't stop thinking about, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I have anything like that. I mean, certainly not... not like a broken record or anything disturbing! I know some people get those awful, intrusive thoughts, but I don't have anything quite so dramatic. \n\nI just... well, my mind just flits from one practical concern to another, like the gutters or the exhibition. It's not that I'm stuck on one terrifying image or anything. I can usually distract myself if I really need to, like if I sit down with a good book or focus on my gardening. \n\nI just wouldn't want you to think I'm losing touch with reality or obsessing over things, Doctor. It's really just ordinary, everyday worries, just... perhaps a few too many of them at once. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining the difference clearly enough...\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stop worrying about everyday things once you start?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose that's actually sort of what brought me in to see you. I hope it doesn't sound too silly, but... yes, it can be a bit tricky to just turn it off. \n\nI really do try to be organized about it! I make my lists, I check all the dates for the exhibition, I make sure I've done everything I reasonably can. And you'd think that would be enough to just put it out of my mind, wouldn't you? But sometimes, even after I've checked everything off, the thoughts just sort of... linger. \n\nIt's not that I'm out of control, certainly not! I just find it a little difficult to fully relax and let it go once I've started thinking about a problem. I keep wondering if I missed something small. Is that a common thing, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm handling things the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about being judged or evaluated by others in social situations?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that's a problem for me. I mean, I enjoy social situations! I have my friends from the gallery, and my book club... I'm quite comfortable around them. \n\nI suppose, like anyone, I like to make a good impression. I want to be a good hostess when I have people over, and I hope they enjoy the food and the company. But I'm certainly not... oh, I don't know, paralyzed by fear of what they're thinking of me. \n\nI just like things to be nice for everyone. It's more about making sure they're comfortable rather than worrying they're judging me. I hope that doesn't make me sound too fussy! I just try to be considerate. Does that seem like a normal way to feel about socializing, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying about everyday things is excessive or out of proportion to the actual situation?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose... perhaps a little bit? I really try not to let things get out of hand, I promise. It's just that sometimes, my mind keeps roaming from one thing to the next. And then, well, these small things start to feel as if they could unravel into something much bigger, if that makes sense? \n\nI really don't want to sound like I'm making a mountain out of a molehill, Doctor. I know I should just take things one step at a time. Do you think it's terribly unusual to get a bit overwhelmed by all those little details? Oh, "interfering" sounds so... drastic. I really don't want you to think I'm just sitting around letting things fall apart, because I'm absolutely not! I still keep up with the garden, and I'm still going to my book club, of course. 

But, well... I suppose if I'm being perfectly honest... I have started arriving a little less prepared for things lately. And I did actually turn down taking on some parts of the exhibition planning this season. It's just that... well, it feels a bit harder to manage all those moving pieces than it used to, with my mind always jumping ahead to the next worry. 

I just thought it would be better to step back a tiny bit rather than do a poor job and let anyone down. That's a responsible way to handle it, don't you think, Doctor? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm giving up on my obligations... I just want to make sure I'm doing what's best for everyone.

I just want to be sure I'm giving you a clear picture, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about everyday things, such as finances, health, or relationships, even when there's no apparent reason to worry?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I try very hard not to be a \"worrywart,\" as they say. I wouldn't want you to think I sit around all day imagining the worst! I really do try to just stay positive and organized. \n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, my mind does tend to wander a bit more than I'd like. Not about anything catastrophic, I promise! Just... well, everyday responsibilities. It's like my mind keeps roaming. I'll be thinking about the exhibition schedule, and then before I know it, I'm fretting over my daughter's travel plans, and then suddenly I'm wondering if the house gutters need fixing. \n\nIt sounds so silly when I say it out loud... but sometimes these small things just start to feel as if they could unravel into something much bigger if I don't stay on top of them. I just like to be prepared and make sure everything is taken care of. That's just being responsible, isn't it, Doctor? I hope I don't sound too foolish...\n\nDoctor: Has your worrying affected your ability to complete tasks at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Oh, I wouldn't say it's affecting my ability to complete things, really. I mean, I'm certainly not dropping the ball on my main responsibilities! I still keep up with the garden, and I never miss my book club, of course. I'd hate for you to think I'm just letting everything slide. \n\nBut... well, I suppose things do take a little more effort lately. I've noticed I'm arriving at book club a bit less prepared than I usually am—just not quite finishing the reading, which is so unlike me. And, um... with the exhibition planning I mentioned, I've actually had to turn down a few of the tasks they asked me to help with. It just... it feels a bit harder to manage all the moving parts than it used to. \n\nI just didn't want to overcommit and let anyone down, you see. I thought it was better to step back slightly rather than do a poor job. That makes sense, doesn't it? I hope you don't think I'm just becoming lazy, Doctor...\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you worry about things more than most people?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I'm not entirely sure how much other people worry, to be perfectly honest. I don't really go around comparing notes with my friends on that sort of thing! I wouldn't want to pry into their private thoughts, and I certainly wouldn't want them thinking I'm overly anxious or... or dramatic. \n\nI suppose... maybe I just have a more active mind? I think some people are just naturally more conscientious, don't you? I like to think of it as being thoughtful rather than, you know, being a worrier. I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing and that everyone is taken care of. \n\nSo, I don't know if it's *more* than most people. I just try to be thorough. I hope that's a helpful answer, Doctor? I'm not really sure how to measure it against anyone else...\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of things in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, yes... I suppose I touched on this a little bit earlier. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, Doctor! But, um, yes, it does seem to cover quite a few different areas. \n\nLike I mentioned before, my mind just sort of keeps roaming around. One minute it's the exhibition schedule, and then it jumps to my daughter's travel plans, and then suddenly I'm worrying over whether the house gutters need fixing. So I guess that is a fairly wide range of topics, isn't it? \n\nIt's just that... well, all those little things from different parts of my life start to feel as if they could unravel into something much bigger if I'm not careful to keep track of them. I just want to make sure everything is running smoothly and everyone is okay. Does that answer your question properly?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts or mental images that you can't stop thinking about, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I have anything like that. I mean, certainly not... not like a broken record or anything disturbing! Or I'll keep getting up to straighten a picture frame or tidy up the coffee table. Not because the house is a mess, I promise! It's just because... well, sitting perfectly quietly seems to make me feel a tiny bit jumpy lately. \n\nIt's nothing terrible, really, I just have a hard time staying entirely still. I hope that doesn't make me sound completely neurotic... I just like to keep things neat, you know? Does that sound like the sort of restlessness you were asking about, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself feeling tense in your muscles, like your shoulders or neck are tight?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I do get a little stiff, but I really think that's just normal for someone my age, you know? I wouldn't want you to think it's anything highly unusual or that I'm complaining about regular aches and pains. \n\nBut... if I really think about it, I guess by the evening, my shoulders and neck do get awfully tight. It's almost like I've been bracing myself all day without realizing it. And, um... sometimes when I sit down to read or work on the plans for the garden center, I'll suddenly notice that I'm clenching my jaw quite hard. \n\nI try to stretch it out, of course, and I don't let it stop me from getting things done. I hope that's not too unusual? Is that the kind of tension you mean, Doctor, or am I just describing normal getting-older stiffness?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself avoiding social interactions because of your worrying?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no... I don't think I avoid people. I really do love seeing my friends, and I wouldn't want you to think I'm becoming some sort of recluse. I still go to my book club, like I mentioned, and I always make sure to call my sister on Sundays. I'd feel just awful if I let those relationships slip.\n\nI mean... I suppose there are times when I might decline a larger dinner party if I'm feeling particularly overwhelmed by everything else on my plate. But that's just because I wouldn't want to be poor company, you know? I'd hate to bring down the mood if my mind is racing about other things. \n\nBut overall, no, I really try my very best to stay connected. I hope that's the right answer? I just want to be sure I'm giving you a clear picture, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about everyday things, such as finances, health, or relationships, even when there's no apparent reason to worry?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I try very hard not to be a \"worrywart,\" as they say. I wouldn't want you to think I sit around all day imagining the worst! I really do try to just stay positive and organized. \n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, my mind does tend to wander a bit more than I'd like. Not about anything catastrophic, I promise! Just... well, everyday responsibilities. It's like my mind keeps roaming. I'll be thinking about the exhibition schedule, and then before I know it, I'm fretting over my daughter's travel plans, and then suddenly I'm wondering if the house gutters need fixing. \n\nIt sounds so silly when I say it out loud... but sometimes these small things just start to feel as if they could unravel into something much bigger if I don't stay on top of them. I just like to be prepared and make sure everything is taken care of. That's just being responsible, isn't it, Doctor? I hope I don't sound too foolish...\n\nDoctor: Has your worrying affected your ability to complete tasks at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Oh, I wouldn't say it's affecting my ability to complete things, really. I mean, I'm certainly not dropping the ball on my main responsibilities! I still keep up with the garden, and I never miss my book club, of course. I'd hate for you to think I'm just letting everything slide. \n\nBut... well, I suppose things do take a little more effort lately. I've noticed I'm arriving at book club a bit less prepared than I usually am—just not quite finishing the reading, which is so unlike me. And, um... with the exhibition planning I mentioned, I've actually had to turn down a few of the tasks they asked me to help with. It just... it feels a bit harder to manage all the moving parts than it used to. \n\nI just didn't want to overcommit and let anyone down, you see. I thought it was better to step back slightly rather than do a poor job. That makes sense, doesn't it? I hope you don't think I'm just becoming lazy, Doctor...\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you worry about things more than most people?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I'm not entirely sure how much other people worry, to be perfectly honest. I don't really go around comparing notes with my friends on that sort of thing! I wouldn't want to pry into their private thoughts, and I certainly wouldn't want them thinking I'm overly anxious or... or dramatic. \n\nI suppose... maybe I just have a more active mind? I think some people are just naturally more conscientious, don't you? I like to think of it as being thoughtful rather than, you know, being a worrier. I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing and that everyone is taken care of. \n\nSo, I don't know if it's *more* than most people. I just try to be thorough. I hope that's a helpful answer, Doctor? I'm not really sure how to measure it against anyone else...\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of things in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, yes... I suppose I touched on this a little bit earlier. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, Doctor! But, um, yes, it does seem to cover quite a few different areas. \n\nLike I mentioned before, my mind just sort of keeps roaming around. One minute it's the exhibition schedule, and then it jumps to my daughter's travel plans, and then suddenly I'm worrying over whether the house gutters need fixing. So I guess that is a fairly wide range of topics, isn't it? \n\nIt's just that... well, all those little things from different parts of my life start to feel as if they could unravel into something much bigger if I'm not careful to keep track of them. I just want to make sure everything is running smoothly and everyone is okay. Does that answer your question properly?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts or mental images that you can't stop thinking about, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I have anything like that. I mean, certainly not... not like a broken record or anything disturbing! I know some people get those awful, intrusive thoughts, but I don't have anything quite so dramatic. \n\nI just... well, my mind just flits from one practical concern to another, like the gutters or the exhibition. It's not that I'm stuck on one terrifying image or anything. I can usually distract myself if I really need to, like if I sit down with a good book or focus on my gardening. \n\nI just wouldn't want you to think I'm losing touch with reality or obsessing over things, Doctor. It's really just ordinary, everyday worries, just... perhaps a few too many of them at once. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining the difference clearly enough...\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stop worrying about everyday things once you start?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose that's actually sort of what brought me in to see you. I hope it doesn't sound too silly, but... yes, it can be a bit tricky to just turn it off. \n\nI really do try to be organized about it! I make my lists, I check all the dates for the exhibition, I make sure I've done everything I reasonably can. And you'd think that would be enough to just put it out of my mind, wouldn't you? But sometimes, even after I've checked everything off, the thoughts just sort of... linger. \n\nIt's not that I'm out of control, certainly not! I just find it a little difficult to fully relax and let it go once I've started thinking about a problem. I keep wondering if I missed something small. Is that a common thing, Doctor? I want to be a good hostess when I have people over, and I hope they enjoy the food and the company. But I'm certainly not... oh, I don't know, paralyzed by fear of what they're thinking of me. \n\nI just like things to be nice for everyone. Well... I suppose if I really think about it, it's been about seven months? Yes, right around there. 

It started shortly after a dear friend of mine passed away. We had a few projects we were involved in together, and... well, I just stepped in to try and keep everything moving forward. I wanted to make sure it was all taken care of properly, so nothing fell through the cracks and nobody else had to stress over it. 

I think that's when my mind just started rushing a bit more than usual, trying to keep track of it all. It's quite natural to feel a little extra pressure when you're trying to hold things together for someone who's gone, isn't it? I just wanted to do the right thing by her, and I hope that makes sense as to why I might be a little more keyed up lately.

I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, my mind does tend to wander a bit more than I'd like. Not about anything catastrophic, I promise! Just... well, everyday responsibilities. It's like my mind keeps roaming. I'll be thinking about the exhibition schedule, and then before I know it, I'm fretting over my daughter's travel plans, and then suddenly I'm wondering if the house gutters need fixing. \n\nIt sounds so silly when I say it out loud... but sometimes these small things just start to feel as if they could unravel into something much bigger if I don't stay on top of them. I just like to be prepared and make sure everything is taken care of. That's just being responsible, isn't it, Doctor? I hope I don't sound too foolish...\n\nDoctor: Has your worrying affected your ability to complete tasks at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Oh, I wouldn't say it's affecting my ability to complete things, really. I mean, I'm certainly not dropping the ball on my main responsibilities! I still keep up with the garden, and I never miss my book club, of course. I'd hate for you to think I'm just letting everything slide. \n\nBut... well, I suppose things do take a little more effort lately. I've noticed I'm arriving at book club a bit less prepared than I usually am—just not quite finishing the reading, which is so unlike me. And, um... with the exhibition planning I mentioned, I've actually had to turn down a few of the tasks they asked me to help with. It just... it feels a bit harder to manage all the moving parts than it used to. \n\nI just didn't want to overcommit and let anyone down, you see. I thought it was better to step back slightly rather than do a poor job. That makes sense, doesn't it? I hope you don't think I'm just becoming lazy, Doctor...\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you worry about things more than most people?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I'm not entirely sure how much other people worry, to be perfectly honest. I don't really go around comparing notes with my friends on that sort of thing! I wouldn't want to pry into their private thoughts, and I certainly wouldn't want them thinking I'm overly anxious or... or dramatic. \n\nI suppose... maybe I just have a more active mind? I think some people are just naturally more conscientious, don't you? I like to think of it as being thoughtful rather than, you know, being a worrier. I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing and that everyone is taken care of. \n\nSo, I don't know if it's *more* than most people. I just try to be thorough. I hope that's a helpful answer, Doctor? I'm not really sure how to measure it against anyone else...\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of things in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, yes... I suppose I touched on this a little bit earlier. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, Doctor! But, um, yes, it does seem to cover quite a few different areas. \n\nLike I mentioned before, my mind just sort of keeps roaming around. One minute it's the exhibition schedule, and then it jumps to my daughter's travel plans, and then suddenly I'm worrying over whether the house gutters need fixing. So I guess that is a fairly wide range of topics, isn't it? \n\nIt's just that... well, all those little things from different parts of my life start to feel as if they could unravel into something much bigger if I'm not careful to keep track of them. I just want to make sure everything is running smoothly and everyone is okay. Does that answer your question properly?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts or mental images that you can't stop thinking about, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I have anything like that. I mean, certainly not... not like a broken record or anything disturbing! I know some people get those awful, intrusive thoughts, but I don't have anything quite so dramatic. \n\nI just... well, my mind just flits from one practical concern to another, like the gutters or the exhibition. It's not that I'm stuck on one terrifying image or anything. I can usually distract myself if I really need to, like if I sit down with a good book or focus on my gardening. \n\nI just wouldn't want you to think I'm losing touch with reality or obsessing over things, Doctor. It's really just ordinary, everyday worries, just... perhaps a few too many of them at once. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining the difference clearly enough...\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stop worrying about everyday things once you start?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose that's actually sort of what brought me in to see you. I hope it doesn't sound too silly, but... yes, it can be a bit tricky to just turn it off. \n\nI really do try to be organized about it! I make my lists, I check all the dates for the exhibition, I make sure I've done everything I reasonably can. And you'd think that would be enough to just put it out of my mind, wouldn't you? But sometimes, even after I've checked everything off, the thoughts just sort of... linger. \n\nIt's not that I'm out of control, certainly not! I just find it a little difficult to fully relax and let it go once I've started thinking about a problem. I keep wondering if I missed something small. Is that a common thing, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm handling things the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about being judged or evaluated by others in social situations?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that's a problem for me. I mean, I enjoy social situations! I have my friends from the gallery, and my book club... I'm quite comfortable around them. \n\nI suppose, like anyone, I like to make a good impression. I want to be a good hostess when I have people over, and I hope they enjoy the food and the company. But I'm certainly not... oh, I don't know, paralyzed by fear of what they're thinking of me. \n\nI just like things to be nice for everyone. It's more about making sure they're comfortable rather than worrying they're judging me. I hope that doesn't make me sound too fussy! I just try to be considerate. Does that seem like a normal way to feel about socializing, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying about everyday things is excessive or out of proportion to the actual situation?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose... perhaps a little bit? I really try not to let things get out of hand, I promise. It's just that sometimes, my mind keeps roaming from one thing to the next. Like, I'll be thinking about the exhibition schedule at the gallery, and then suddenly I'm worrying about my daughter's travel plans, and then wondering if the house gutters need fixing before it rains... \n\nI know they're just normal, everyday things, but sometimes they all sort of pile up. And then, well, these small things start to feel as if they could unravel into something much bigger, if that makes sense? \n\nI really don't want to sound like I'm making a mountain out of a molehill, Doctor. I know I should just take things one step at a time. Do you think it's terribly unusual to get a bit overwhelmed by all those little details? I just want to be sure I'm keeping a proper perspective on things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is interfering with your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: Oh, \"interfering\" sounds so... drastic. I really don't want you to think I'm just sitting around letting things fall apart, because I'm absolutely not! I still keep up with the garden, and I'm still going to my book club, of course. \n\nBut, well... I suppose if I'm being perfectly honest... I have started arriving a little less prepared for things lately. And I did actually turn down taking on some parts of the exhibition planning this season. I just want to make sure I'm doing what's best for everyone.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this persistent worrying?\n\nPatient: Well... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, I certainly wouldn't say I'm unhappy. I have a wonderful life, truly, and I'd hate to sound ungrateful! 

But... well, I suppose, like I was mentioning just a moment ago... the fact that I've had to turn down parts of the exhibition planning, and realizing I'm showing up to my book club a bit less prepared than I usually would... it does take a little bit of the joy out of those things. I am still keeping up with the garden and making sure I get out of the house, I promise! I'm not just moping about. 

It's just that... because everything feels so much harder to manage than it used to, it does weigh on my mood a little bit. It makes me feel a bit less capable, which I suppose dampens my spirits. I just want to feel like I'm doing a good job with my life and my responsibilities, you know? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm complaining too much... it's quite normal to feel a bit down when you aren't performing at your best, isn't it?

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello, Doctor. Thank you so much for taking the time to see me. I, um... I mean, I'll sit down, I'll make my lists, I'll double-check all the dates for whatever is coming up, and I'll do absolutely everything I reasonably can to handle things. But even after all that, my mind just keeps fretting over it and I can't seem to shut it off. \n\nI hope I'm explaining that right... does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a sudden, intense feeling of fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. Not like a sudden attack or anything like that. I mean, I get nervous, of course, but it's more of a... a slow, steady hum in the background, rather than a big explosion of fear. \n\nI wouldn't want you to think I'm having, you know, those sort of dramatic episodes. I really try to keep things together. Is... is that the sort of thing you were looking for? I hope I'm answering that clearly enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain situations or activities because of your worrying?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I wouldn't say I completely avoid things. I really try my best to stay active and keep up with my commitments, I promise. I still keep up with the garden, of course, and I always go to my book club. I also help with some local exhibition planning, and I've actually started turning down certain parts of that. Not because I don't care! I really do try to stay on top of things. Does that... does that answer your question, Doctor? I wouldn't want to overstate things and make it sound worse than it is. But... yes, I suppose I do find myself feeling a little wound up sometimes, even when I'm just trying to relax in my own living room. \n\nI'll notice that I'm tapping my fingers the whole time I'm on a phone call with a friend. Or I'll keep getting up to straighten a picture frame or tidy up the coffee table. Not because the house is a mess, I promise! It's just because... well, sitting perfectly quietly seems to make me feel a tiny bit jumpy lately. \n\nIt's nothing terrible, really, I just have a hard time staying entirely still. I hope that doesn't make me sound completely neurotic... I just like to keep things neat, you know? I suppose I do get a little stiff, but I really think that's just normal for someone my age, you know? I wouldn't want you to think it's anything highly unusual or that I'm complaining about regular aches and pains. \n\nBut... if I really think about it, I guess by the evening, my shoulders and neck do get awfully tight. It's almost like I've been bracing myself all day without realizing it. And, um... sometimes when I sit down to read or work on the plans for the garden center, I'll suddenly notice that I'm clenching my jaw quite hard. \n\nI try to stretch it out, of course, and I don't let it stop me from getting things done. I hope that's not too unusual? Is that the kind of tension you mean, Doctor, or am I just describing normal getting-older stiffness?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself avoiding social interactions because of your worrying?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no... I don't think I avoid people. I really do love seeing my friends, and I wouldn't want you to think I'm becoming some sort of recluse. I just want to be sure I'm giving you a clear picture, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about everyday things, such as finances, health, or relationships, even when there's no apparent reason to worry?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I try very hard not to be a \"worrywart,\" as they say. I wouldn't want you to think I sit around all day imagining the worst! I really do try to just stay positive and organized. \n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, my mind does tend to wander a bit more than I'd like. Not about anything catastrophic, I promise! Just... well, everyday responsibilities. It's like my mind keeps roaming. I'll be thinking about the exhibition schedule, and then before I know it, I'm fretting over my daughter's travel plans, and then suddenly I'm wondering if the house gutters need fixing. \n\nIt sounds so silly when I say it out loud... but sometimes these small things just start to feel as if they could unravel into something much bigger if I don't stay on top of them. I just like to be prepared and make sure everything is taken care of. That's just being responsible, isn't it, Doctor? I hope I don't sound too foolish...\n\nDoctor: Has your worrying affected your ability to complete tasks at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Oh, I wouldn't say it's affecting my ability to complete things, really. I mean, I'm certainly not dropping the ball on my main responsibilities! I still keep up with the garden, and I never miss my book club, of course. I'd hate for you to think I'm just letting everything slide. \n\nBut... well, I suppose things do take a little more effort lately. I've noticed I'm arriving at book club a bit less prepared than I usually am—just not quite finishing the reading, which is so unlike me. And, um... with the exhibition planning I mentioned, I've actually had to turn down a few of the tasks they asked me to help with. It just... it feels a bit harder to manage all the moving parts than it used to. \n\nI just didn't want to overcommit and let anyone down, you see. I thought it was better to step back slightly rather than do a poor job. That makes sense, doesn't it? I hope you don't think I'm just becoming lazy, Doctor...\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you worry about things more than most people?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I'm not entirely sure how much other people worry, to be perfectly honest. I don't really go around comparing notes with my friends on that sort of thing! I wouldn't want to pry into their private thoughts, and I certainly wouldn't want them thinking I'm overly anxious or... or dramatic. \n\nI suppose... maybe I just have a more active mind? I think some people are just naturally more conscientious, don't you? I like to think of it as being thoughtful rather than, you know, being a worrier. I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing and that everyone is taken care of. \n\nSo, I don't know if it's *more* than most people. I just try to be thorough. I hope that's a helpful answer, Doctor? I'm not really sure how to measure it against anyone else...\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of things in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, yes... I suppose I touched on this a little bit earlier. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, Doctor! But, um, yes, it does seem to cover quite a few different areas. \n\nLike I mentioned before, my mind just sort of keeps roaming around. One minute it's the exhibition schedule, and then it jumps to my daughter's travel plans, and then suddenly I'm worrying over whether the house gutters need fixing. So I guess that is a fairly wide range of topics, isn't it? \n\nIt's just that... well, all those little things from different parts of my life start to feel as if they could unravel into something much bigger if I'm not careful to keep track of them. I just want to make sure everything is running smoothly and everyone is okay. Does that answer your question properly?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts or mental images that you can't stop thinking about, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I have anything like that. I mean, certainly not... not like a broken record or anything disturbing! I know some people get those awful, intrusive thoughts, but I don't have anything quite so dramatic. \n\nI just... well, my mind just flits from one practical concern to another, like the gutters or the exhibition. It's not that I'm stuck on one terrifying image or anything. I can usually distract myself if I really need to, like if I sit down with a good book or focus on my gardening. \n\nI just wouldn't want you to think I'm losing touch with reality or obsessing over things, Doctor. It's really just ordinary, everyday worries, just... perhaps a few too many of them at once. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining the difference clearly enough...\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stop worrying about everyday things once you start?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose that's actually sort of what brought me in to see you. I hope it doesn't sound too silly, but... yes, it can be a bit tricky to just turn it off. \n\nI really do try to be organized about it! I make my lists, I check all the dates for the exhibition, I make sure I've done everything I reasonably can. And you'd think that would be enough to just put it out of my mind, wouldn't you? I just find it a little difficult to fully relax and let it go once I've started thinking about a problem. I keep wondering if I missed something small. Is that a common thing, Doctor? I'll be thinking about the exhibition schedule, and then before I know it, I'm fretting over my daughter's travel plans, and then suddenly I'm wondering if the house gutters need fixing. \n\nIt sounds so silly when I say it out loud... but sometimes these small things just start to feel as if they could unravel into something much bigger if I don't stay on top of them. I just like to be prepared and make sure everything is taken care of. That's just being responsible, isn't it, Doctor? I hope I don't sound too foolish...\n\nDoctor: Has your worrying affected your ability to complete tasks at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Oh, I wouldn't say it's affecting my ability to complete things, really. I mean, I'm certainly not dropping the ball on my main responsibilities! I still keep up with the garden, and I never miss my book club, of course. I'd hate for you to think I'm just letting everything slide. \n\nBut... well, I suppose things do take a little more effort lately. I've noticed I'm arriving at book club a bit less prepared than I usually am—just not quite finishing the reading, which is so unlike me. And, um... with the exhibition planning I mentioned, I've actually had to turn down a few of the tasks they asked me to help with. It just... it feels a bit harder to manage all the moving parts than it used to. \n\nI just didn't want to overcommit and let anyone down, you see. I thought it was better to step back slightly rather than do a poor job. That makes sense, doesn't it? I hope you don't think I'm just becoming lazy, Doctor...\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you worry about things more than most people?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I'm not entirely sure how much other people worry, to be perfectly honest. I don't really go around comparing notes with my friends on that sort of thing! I wouldn't want to pry into their private thoughts, and I certainly wouldn't want them thinking I'm overly anxious or... or dramatic. \n\nI suppose... maybe I just have a more active mind? I think some people are just naturally more conscientious, don't you? I like to think of it as being thoughtful rather than, you know, being a worrier. I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing and that everyone is taken care of. \n\nSo, I don't know if it's *more* than most people. I just try to be thorough. I hope that's a helpful answer, Doctor? I'm not really sure how to measure it against anyone else...\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about a wide range of things in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, yes... I suppose I touched on this a little bit earlier. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, Doctor! But, um, yes, it does seem to cover quite a few different areas. \n\nLike I mentioned before, my mind just sort of keeps roaming around. One minute it's the exhibition schedule, and then it jumps to my daughter's travel plans, and then suddenly I'm worrying over whether the house gutters need fixing. So I guess that is a fairly wide range of topics, isn't it? \n\nIt's just that... well, all those little things from different parts of my life start to feel as if they could unravel into something much bigger if I'm not careful to keep track of them. I just want to make sure everything is running smoothly and everyone is okay. Does that answer your question properly?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any recurring thoughts or mental images that you can't stop thinking about, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I have anything like that. I mean, certainly not... not like a broken record or anything disturbing! I know some people get those awful, intrusive thoughts, but I don't have anything quite so dramatic. \n\nI just... well, my mind just flits from one practical concern to another, like the gutters or the exhibition. It's not that I'm stuck on one terrifying image or anything. I can usually distract myself if I really need to, like if I sit down with a good book or focus on my gardening. \n\nI just wouldn't want you to think I'm losing touch with reality or obsessing over things, Doctor. It's really just ordinary, everyday worries, just... perhaps a few too many of them at once. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining the difference clearly enough...\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stop worrying about everyday things once you start?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose that's actually sort of what brought me in to see you. I hope it doesn't sound too silly, but... yes, it can be a bit tricky to just turn it off. \n\nI really do try to be organized about it! I make my lists, I check all the dates for the exhibition, I make sure I've done everything I reasonably can. And you'd think that would be enough to just put it out of my mind, wouldn't you? But sometimes, even after I've checked everything off, the thoughts just sort of... linger. \n\nIt's not that I'm out of control, certainly not! I just find it a little difficult to fully relax and let it go once I've started thinking about a problem. I keep wondering if I missed something small. Is that a common thing, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm handling things the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about being judged or evaluated by others in social situations?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that's a problem for me. I mean, I enjoy social situations! I have my friends from the gallery, and my book club... I'm quite comfortable around them. \n\nI suppose, like anyone, I like to make a good impression. I want to be a good hostess when I have people over, and I hope they enjoy the food and the company. But I'm certainly not... oh, I don't know, paralyzed by fear of what they're thinking of me. \n\nI just like things to be nice for everyone. It's more about making sure they're comfortable rather than worrying they're judging me. I hope that doesn't make me sound too fussy! I just try to be considerate.
